                10 Quaternion-to-frame time offset Every IMU-based gate and prior Correlate IMU rate with optical flow §6.1.7
                11 Link range and failsafe behaviour in the lot Safety: link loss behind concrete; RTH without GNSS may become ATTI drift and an automatic landing Walk the lot with the drone on the ground or in a low hover with a spotter; set the Signal Lost Action to Hover or Land §1.5.8, §1.6

                7.2Engineering unknowns that decide primary vs fallback#

                • FTheta refit residual. gsplat's FTheta model lacks DJI's tangential terms. If the refit residual is above ~0.3 px, pre-warp the images or fall back to the virtual-view splat (§6.1.3, §6.8.2).
                • Splat max_angle. The Phase-0a sweep {80°, 90°, 97°, 103°} on FIORD and then on drone data decides whether the raw-fisheye splat can be trusted beyond 80° off-axis, or whether the rim band stays with virtual views (§5.8, §6.9).
                • GOFish on 16 GB. GOFish was trained on a 24 GB GPU and is CC BY-NC. Its value as the experimental splat (and as Option B-fish) depends on fitting 16 GB and beating the primary by ≥ 1.5 dB (§5.7, §6.6).
                • Whether 3DGRUT's fisheye path renders θ > 90°. Not confirmed (§5.2). It matters only as an alternative to gsplat.
                • Code availability for Seam360GS and DirectFisheye-GS. No code was found (§5.7).
                • Dense completeness on bare concrete. Whether OpenMVS reaches about 70% floor completeness. If not, PGSR or 2DGS with planar priors outside evaluated regions (§4.4, §6.8.2).
                • Reference accuracy for long check lines. A distance-only trilateration network may not support a 0.03% claim over 50 m. Measure long lines directly, or borrow a total station (§6.3).
                • Dense evidence on garage-like scenes. ETH3D indoor and ScanNet++ numbers now exist for COLMAP, ACMM/ACMMP, PGSR, GOF, 2DGS and DN-Splatter (§4.4). An OpenMVS-specific ETH3D value is still UNVERIFIED, and no benchmark resembles a dim, compressed, textureless garage.

                7.3Research gaps and possible contributions#

                1. Direction-aware, per-sector frame curation with partial rig frames (main gap). No published method scores quality per fisheye sector, cubemap face or virtual view and then feeds partial frames to rig SfM. The proposal: an IMU-predicted blur map on the sphere, fused with relative sharpness, which emits per-sector masks (§3.2, §3.10, §6.9.1).
                2. A metric study of the seam band. Compare the raw-fisheye 10-view rig, the stitched-equirect rig (seam masked and unmasked) and native spherical SfM, with checkpoints stratified by elevation so seam-band error is measured directly (§6.9.1).
                3. A "garage doppelganger" benchmark. Marker IDs give free ground-truth labels for true and false revisits, so loop gates can be scored by precision and recall: marker-ID, rig consensus, gravity, Doppelgangers, and incremental-vs-global disagreement (§4.2, §6.9.1).
                4. Self-calibrating fisheye-coordinate BA with an IMU rolling-shutter term for an up/down dual-fisheye rig (Option C, §6.7).
                5. Splatting beyond 180° on real drone data. FTheta-UT on raw fisheye vs virtual pinhole, max_angle sweeps, depth-prior choice, and PPISP vs bilateral grid, with metrics reported per region (poles vs rim) (§5.7, §6.9.1).
                6. Evidence gaps the project could fill as by-products: whether no-reference IQA scores predict SfM success at all; principled frames-per-metre for 360° indoor capture; robust (distractor-aware) splatting losses on fisheye input; how selection density affects false-loop rates; whether HEVC I/P frames carry more matchable texture than B-frames (§3.10).
                7. Dataset release. Avata 360 OSV, calibration clip, marker coordinates and laser distances. ORBIT shows that SfM on real 360° video still fails often, so a metric indoor 360° drone dataset would be useful to others [see Ch. 4].

                Glossary

                Terms marked with a dotted underline in the text show these definitions on hover, focus or tap.

                2DGS (2D Gaussian Splatting)
                A splatting variant that uses flat, disk-like Gaussians aligned to surfaces, giving better-defined geometry than 3D ellipsoids; gsplat ships its own Apache-licensed rasterizer for it (§4.4, §6.6).
                Absolute trajectory error (ATE)
                RMSE of position differences between an estimated trajectory and ground truth after rigid or Sim(3) alignment; measures global consistency.
                Accuracy
                Closeness of a measurement to the true value; includes bias. Contrast with precision.
                Adaptive density control
                The 3DGS procedure that clones, splits and prunes Gaussians during training based on gradients, size and opacity.
                Alpha compositing
                Front-to-back blending $C=\sum_k c_k\alpha_k\prod_{j<k}(1-\alpha_j)$ used by both NeRF and 3DGS.
                Appearance embedding
                A learned per-image vector or colour transform that absorbs exposure, white-balance and lighting differences so they do not corrupt geometry.
                AprilTag
                A family of square fiducial markers with binary IDs (e.g. tag36h11), detectable with sub-pixel corners; used here as control points and checkpoints (§4.3).
                ATTI mode
                DJI's attitude-only flight mode, entered when neither GNSS nor vision positioning is available; the drone holds attitude but not position and drifts with air movement (§1.5.8).
                Back-projection
                Mapping a pixel to the 3D ray of points that project onto it; depth is unknown without more information.
                Baseline
                Distance between two camera centres; larger baselines give better-conditioned triangulation but harder matching.
                Bearing vector
                Unit 3D direction of a pixel's ray; the natural measurement for spherical cameras.
                Bilateral grid
                A low-resolution 3D grid of affine colour transforms indexed by pixel position and intensity, used to model per-image tone/exposure changes.
                Bundle adjustment (BA)
                Joint non-linear least-squares refinement of camera parameters and 3D points that minimises (robust) reprojection error.
                Camera matrix
                The $3\times4$ matrix $\mathbf{P}=\mathbf{K}[\mathbf{R}\mid\mathbf{t}]$ that maps homogeneous world points to homogeneous pixels.
                Chamfer distance
                Symmetric average nearest-neighbour distance between two point sets; a combined accuracy/completeness score.
                Checkpoint (CP)
                A surveyed point withheld from the adjustment and used only to measure accuracy.
                Completeness
                Fraction of ground-truth surface within a threshold of the reconstruction (recall).
                Cubemap
                Six 90° perspective images on the faces of a cube, used to represent a full sphere with pinhole cameras.
                Densification
                Adding Gaussians (by cloning or splitting) in under- or over-reconstructed regions.
                Depth fusion
                Merging per-view depth maps into one consistent point cloud or volume.
                Distortion model
                Parametric correction (radial, tangential, fisheye) for deviation of a real lens from the pinhole model.
                D-Log M
                DJI's flat, 10-bit log colour profile, which preserves dynamic range for later grading; recommended for capture here (§1.1, §1.6).
                Doppelganger (visual aliasing)
                A pair of images of different places that look alike (identical pillars or bays) and so produce consistent but wrong matches; the cause of false loop closures (§3.4, §4.2).
                Double Sphere model
                A fisheye camera model with two unit spheres and a closed-form inverse, able to represent fields of view beyond 180° [203] (§1.2.1).
                Drift
                Accumulation of small pose errors over a trajectory, causing growing global error and scale inconsistency.
                D-SSIM
                $1-\mathrm{SSIM}$ (often halved), a structural-similarity loss term used with L1 in 3DGS.
                DUSt3R
                A transformer (CVPR 2024) that regresses dense pointmaps for an image pair in a common frame, from which poses and depth follow.
                Epipolar line / great circle
                The locus in image 2 where a point from image 1 must appear; a line for pinhole cameras, a great circle for spherical ones.
                Equirectangular projection (ERP)
                Panorama format mapping longitude and latitude linearly to image columns and rows.
                Essential matrix
                $\mathbf{E}=[\mathbf{t}]_\times\mathbf{R}$, encoding calibrated relative pose up to scale; satisfies $\hat{\mathbf{x}}_2^\top\mathbf{E}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_1=0$.
                EUCM (Enhanced Unified Camera Model)
                A wide-angle camera model that projects through a sphere/ellipsoid; mathematically supports θ > 90°, but COLMAP's implementation requires positive depth (Editorial notes; §4.1).
                EWA splatting
                Elliptical-weighted-average projection of 3D Gaussian kernels to 2D via a local affine approximation; the basis of 3DGS rasterisation.
                Extrinsics
                Rotation and translation relating world and camera coordinates.
                Feed-forward reconstruction
                Predicting cameras and geometry in one network pass without per-scene optimisation (DUSt3R, VGGT, MapAnything).
                Fisheye lens
                Very wide-angle lens (≥180° possible) modelled by mapping ray angle to image radius rather than by a distorted pinhole.
                Floater
                A spurious semi-transparent primitive in free space that explains view-specific effects in training images.
                F-score
                Harmonic mean of precision and recall at a distance threshold.
                FTheta model
                A fisheye model mapping the off-axis angle θ to image radius by a polynomial; gsplat's ftheta camera with the unscented transform can render θ > 90° (§5.2, §6.1.3).
                Fundamental matrix
                Uncalibrated counterpart of the essential matrix, $\mathbf{F}=\mathbf{K}_2^{-\top}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{K}_1^{-1}$, 7 DoF.
                Gauge freedom
                The 7-DoF similarity ambiguity (position, orientation, scale) under which BA's cost is invariant; must be fixed externally.
                Gaussian Splatting (3DGS)
                Scene representation as anisotropic 3D Gaussians with opacity and SH colour, optimised through a differentiable tile rasteriser (Kerbl et al., SIGGRAPH 2023).
                Global SfM
                SfM that solves all camera rotations and positions jointly (rotation/translation averaging or global positioning) before one BA.
                GLOMAP
                Global SfM system (ECCV 2024) integrated into COLMAP since 4.0.
                Ground control point (GCP)
                Surveyed point used inside the adjustment to fix datum and scale and limit drift.
                Hash encoding
                Instant-NGP's multiresolution grids of trainable features stored in hash tables.
                Homogeneous coordinates
                Coordinates with an extra component, defined up to scale, that make projective maps linear.
                Homography
                $3\times3$ projective map between two images of a plane, or between images under pure rotation.
                Horizon band / rim band
                On this up/down dual-fisheye camera, the outer ring of each fisheye (≈ 80–105° off-axis), which images the horizon: walls, pillars, cars, and the stitch seam (§1.2, §3.2).
                Incremental SfM
                SfM that grows the reconstruction one image at a time with PnP, triangulation and repeated BA (COLMAP's default).
                Intrinsics
                Internal camera parameters: focal length, principal point, skew and distortion.
                Kannala–Brandt (KB) model
                A generic fisheye model expressing the distorted angle as an odd polynomial in θ; DJI's per-unit calibration is a KB-type model with five radial and two tangential terms (§1.2.1).
                Levenberg–Marquardt
                Damped Gauss–Newton method interpolating between Gauss–Newton and gradient descent; the standard BA solver.
                LightGlue
                Adaptive attention-based keypoint matcher (ICCV 2023), successor to SuperGlue.
                Loop closure
                Recognising a revisited place and adding a constraint that corrects accumulated drift; false ones fold the map.
                MASt3R
                DUSt3R extension (ECCV 2024) with dense descriptors for 3D-grounded matching.
                MCMC densification
                A 3DGS training strategy that treats Gaussians as samples of a Markov chain Monte Carlo process, relocating low-opacity Gaussians instead of the clone/split heuristic, with a fixed budget (§5.4).
                Mip-NeRF 360
                NeRF variant (CVPR 2022) for unbounded scenes captured by orbiting cameras; "360" does not mean panoramic input.
                Multi-view stereo (MVS)
                Dense per-pixel depth estimation from many calibrated images.
                NeRF
                Neural radiance field: an MLP mapping position and direction to density and colour, rendered by volume rendering.
                Neural SDF
                A network representing a signed distance function, rendered via an SDF-to-density mapping (NeuS, VolSDF).
                Opacity reset
                Periodically lowering all Gaussian opacities during 3DGS training so unneeded Gaussians can be pruned.
                OpenMVS
                An open-source dense-reconstruction library (densify, mesh, refine, texture), AGPL-3.0 licensed; the metric dense baseline here (§4.4).
                ORB
                Fast binary feature (oriented FAST + rotated BRIEF) used in ORB-SLAM.
                OSV
                DJI's 360° video container; on the Avata 360 an MP4 holding two unstitched fisheye HEVC streams plus protobuf metadata (calibration and attitude) (§1.4.1).
                Partial rig frame
                A rig frame in which only a subset of the virtual views is used (others masked or dropped); COLMAP's rig model allows this (§3.2).
                PatchMatch
                Randomised propagate-and-refine algorithm for dense correspondence, used for per-pixel depth/normal estimation in MVS.
                Perspective-n-Point (PnP)
                Estimating camera pose from known 3D points and their 2D projections.
                Photo-consistency
                The assumption that a surface point looks the same in all views; violated by specular, textureless or moving surfaces.
                Pinhole model
                Ideal central projection through a single point onto an image plane.
                Pointmap
                Per-pixel 3D coordinates in a chosen frame, the output representation of DUSt3R-family models.
                Poisson surface reconstruction
                Fitting a smooth indicator function to oriented points and extracting its level set as a watertight mesh.
                Pose graph
                Graph of camera poses with relative-pose constraints, optimised to distribute loop-closure corrections.
                PPISP
                An appearance-compensation module (per-sensor vignetting and response, per-frame exposure and white balance, with a controller for novel views) built into gsplat and 3DGRUT [40] (§5.3).
                Precision (survey sense)
                Repeatability or spread of estimates, independent of bias.
                RANSAC
                Random sample consensus: robust model fitting by repeated minimal-sample hypotheses and inlier counting.
                Relative pose error (RPE)
                Error in relative motion over a fixed interval; measures local drift.
                Reprojection error
                Pixel distance between an observed feature and the projection of its 3D point; measures self-consistency, not accuracy.
                Rig
                Several cameras with fixed, known (or jointly estimated) relative poses, reconstructed as one unit.
                Rolling shutter
                Sensor readout row by row, so different image rows are captured at different times; causes skew under fast motion.
                Rotation averaging
                Estimating absolute camera rotations from many noisy relative rotations.
                Scale ambiguity
                Monocular reconstructions are recoverable only up to a global scale factor.
                Scale bar
                A measured distance between two targets, used as a soft distance constraint in bundle adjustment to fix scale (§4.3).
                Schur complement
                Algebraic elimination of point variables in BA, leaving a smaller reduced camera system.
                Seam parallax
                The misalignment at a stitch seam caused by the two lenses not sharing an optical centre; roughly baseline divided by distance, so it grows at close range (§1.2.2).
                SIFT
                Scale-invariant feature transform: DoG keypoints with 128-D gradient-histogram descriptors.
                Sim(3)
                The 7-DoF group of similarity transforms (rotation, translation, uniform scale).
                SLAM
                Simultaneous localisation and mapping: online, causal estimation of trajectory and map from a sensor stream.
                Spherical harmonics (SH)
                Orthonormal basis on the sphere; 3DGS stores view-dependent colour as SH coefficients (degree 3 by default).
                Structure from Motion (SfM)
                Recovering camera poses and sparse 3D structure from overlapping images.
                SuperPoint
                Self-supervised CNN for joint keypoint detection and description.
                Survey frame (S)
                The single metric coordinate frame defined by the markers after control bundle adjustment; mesh, splat and checkpoints all live in it (§6.1.4).
                Tile-based rasterisation
                Splitting the screen into tiles, sorting overlapping Gaussians per tile by depth, and compositing per pixel in parallel.
                Transmittance
                Fraction of light surviving along a ray up to a point, $T=\exp(-\int\sigma)$ or $\prod(1-\alpha)$.
                Triangulation
                Estimating a 3D point from its projections in two or more views with known poses.
                Trilateration network
                A set of measured distances between markers, adjusted together to give marker coordinates without a total station (§4.3, §6.3).
                TSDF
                Truncated signed distance function accumulated in a voxel grid to fuse depth maps; meshed with marching cubes.
                Unscented transform
                Propagating a Gaussian through a non-linear function by mapping sigma points and refitting; used by 3DGUT for exact camera models.
                Virtual view
                A pinhole image resampled from a raw fisheye along a chosen viewing direction; the 10 virtual views of one frame form a rig frame (§4.1).
                Vocabulary tree
                Hierarchical quantisation of descriptors into visual words for fast image retrieval and loop detection.
                Volume rendering
                Integrating density-weighted colour along a ray to produce a pixel value.
